                        Reference is made to my memorandum of September 23,Tele. Roo._
             1957, on the captioned matter which reflected an Intelli.-      ~1;"'"'•-
             gence Advisory Committee (IAC) evaluation of an Air Defense , r           :;z;
             Command (.ADC) report that radar stations at Montauk Point, t/           :~
             Long Island, New York, and Benton, Pennsylvania, detected an\i.,...~ '{.iA..tl
             unidentified object proce.:ding in a westward direction with            ·-
             an al~itude of 50,000 feet and a speed of 2,000 knots                 I
             (approximately 2,300 miles per hour) on September 20, 1957.
             The IAC intelligence evaluation of this report reflected
             that "It is highly improbable that a Soviet operation is ~
             responsible for the unidentified flying object reports of                ~_j-1
             September 20, 1957."                                        .                              1\
            ··. .. -,_-;Today, at an Executive Session of the IAC,
          General Millard Lewis 1 Director, Air Force Intelligence,    ,
                                                                                                    ·   lJ
          advised that although the .ADC has not completed its investi~
          gation of the evidence on this matter, there are continuing
           indications that the object detected was an atmospheric
             l
          phenomenon. The radar pickups now reflect speed variations
           in the object's course, ranging from 1,500 miles per hour to
          4,500 miles per hour. This latter speed is improbable
          according to U. B. scientific theory for any type flying
          object which this could conceivably be. General Lewis added
         ~that the present sun spots are associated with the peculiar
           radar activity throughout the globe and that this· could have
           some cause for the captioned report.
                 ACTION:
                           Liaison will report further information on this
                 matter as quickly as it is developed.

                  The followtng informatton was obtatned by Liaison
        Supervisor Jl. w. Ku.hrtz at the Intelligence Advisory Committee - Watch.
        Committee·spectal mee~ing on September 21, 1957, relattve to the
        captioned matter. The tnttial report in this matter was given by the
        U.S. Air ~efense Command 10 the White Hou~ on September 20, 1957,
        reflecting ~at an unident~J~ed flying object was picked up by radar
        station at Montauk Point, Long Island, New York, at approximately
        4 p.m., proceeding in a westward dtrection with an altitude of
        50,000 feet and a speed of 2,000 knots (approximately 2300 miles per
        hour). The radar station at Montauk Point maintained an image for
        approximately one minute, and thereafter it was picked up by a radar
        station at Benton, Pennsylvania (Wilkes-Barre, Pennsylvanta), which
        coinctded with the .Montauk Po tnt ptckup and .was tracked by. Een~on for
        approximately 9 minutes. Thereafter, jamming was reported by several
        radar stations westward as far as Chicago.
                    The Watch Committee at its meeting on September 21 was told
        bj,t the Air Defense Command that the odginal reports had been down-
        graded, stnce there was an 11-minute break tn the tracktng by the
        vartous radalt. stat tons, and further that weather ::stcitions; in ~e
        ar.ea weTe of the type wh i.ch have in the past produced false radar
        "p'ips" and electronic information. Also, there were-a number of
        Strategic .Air Command planes in the Chicago area on a training flight.
        The Watch Committee was told that the Air Defense Command has not
        completed its investigatton of this incident; however, in any event
        it; was reported that the phenomena reported west of Buffalo, New York,
        were not related to the object as reported by JJontau.k Point art'd
        Benton, Pennsylvania.               '·
                   The Watch Committee concluded that, "It is highly improba.!!J..G--
         that a Soviet operation is responsible for the unidentified flying--}
       \ object reports of September, 1957.    The 'llatch Committee in consideltng
                                            11

         thts matter also concluded that there is no tntelligence on Soviet
         activities which can be related to a missile launching of thts ~~!

over the U.S. It is unli~ely that a Soviet aircraft could conduct
a mission at this speed and altttude and return to Souiet territory.

for launching a one way missile on an operation over the U.S., and

Committee ruled out one way reconnaissance operutions on the li~elihood
